        "Definition": "<b>Definition of Disability</b><br/><br/>As a general matter, \"disability\" for legal purposes means a physical or mental impairment that substantially limits one or more major life activities, or having a record of such an impairment, or being regarded as having such an impairment.<br/><br/>Major life activities includes things like caring for oneself, performing manual tasks, seeing, hearing, eating, sleeping, walking, standing, lifting, bending, speaking, breathing, learning, reading, concentrating, thinking, communicating, working, and the operation of a major bodily function (like the immune system, normal cell growth, or digestive, bowel, bladder, neurological, brain, respiratory, circulatory, endocrine and/or reproductive functions). An impairment that is episodic or in remission is a disability if it would substantially limit a major life activity when active.<br/><br/>The determination of whether an impairment substantially limits a major life activity is made without regard to the ameliorative effects of mitigating measures (such as medication, medical supplies, equipment, or appliances, some low-vision devices (see below), prosthetics including limbs and devices, hearing aids and cochlear implants or other implantable hearing devices, mobility devices, oxygen therapy equipment and supplies; use of assistive technology, reasonable accommodations or auxiliary aids or services, or learned behavioral or adaptive neurological modifications). <i>Provided, however</i>, that the mitigating impact of ordinary eyeglasses or contact lenses <i>shall</i> be considered in determining whether an impairment substantially limits a major life activity. Alcohol or drug addiction are generally considered disabilities, however: the current use of drugs or alcohol does not qualify as a disability if it prevents the individual from performing the duties of the job or if their employment would, due to the drug or alcohol use, constitute a direct threat to property or the safety of others.",

          "DisabilityConfigurable": {
            "Title": "Voluntary Self-Identification of Disability",
            "Description": "To facilitate compliance obligations, we ask all candidates if they would voluntarily self-identify their disability status. Providing this information is optional and there will be no effect on your application regardless of what you elect. Information related to a disability will be kept confidential, maintained separately from other application records, and used only in ways permitted by applicable law. A definition of disability for this purpose is available __here__.",
            "DefinitionLinkText": "here",
            "Definition": {
              "Header": "Definition of Disability",
              "Paragraph1": "As a general matter, \"disability\" for legal purposes means a physical or mental impairment that substantially limits one or more major life activities, or having a record of such an impairment, or being regarded as having such an impairment.",
              "Paragraph2": "Major life activities includes things like caring for oneself, performing manual tasks, seeing, hearing, eating, sleeping, walking, standing, lifting, bending, speaking, breathing, learning, reading, concentrating, thinking, communicating, working, and the operation of a major bodily function (like the immune system, normal cell growth, or digestive, bowel, bladder, neurological, brain, respiratory, circulatory, endocrine and/or reproductive functions). An impairment that is episodic or in remission is a disability if it would substantially limit a major life activity when active.",
              "Paragraph3": "The determination of whether an impairment substantially limits a major life activity is made without regard to the ameliorative effects of mitigating measures (such as medication, medical supplies, equipment, or appliances, some low-vision devices (see below), prosthetics including limbs and devices, hearing aids and cochlear implants or other implantable hearing devices, mobility devices, oxygen therapy equipment and supplies; use of assistive technology, reasonable accommodations or auxiliary aids or services, or learned behavioral or adaptive neurological modifications). <i>Provided, however</i>, that the mitigating impact of ordinary eyeglasses or contact lenses <i>shall</i> be considered in determining whether an impairment substantially limits a major life activity. Alcohol or drug addiction are generally considered disabilities, however: the current use of drugs or alcohol does not qualify as a disability if it prevents the individual from performing the duties of the job or if their employment would, due to the drug or alcohol use, constitute a direct threat to property or the safety of others."
            }
